

======================================================================
WIKIPEDIA ARTICLE: 2024 in American television
======================================================================

{{Short description|none}}
{{use mdy dates|date=October 2024}}
<!-- "none" is preferred when the title is sufficiently descriptive; see [[WP:SDNONE]] -->
{{USTV year|2024}}
In [[Television in the United States|American television]] in 2024, notable events included television show debuts, finales, and cancellations; channel launches, closures, and re-brandings; stations changing or adding their network affiliations; information on controversies, business transactions, and [[carriage disputes]]; and deaths of those who made various contributions to the medium.

== Notable events ==
=== January ===
{| class="wikitable sortable"
! Date
! Event
! Ref.

|-
! 4
| The [[National Collegiate Athletic Association|NCAA]] and [[ESPN]] announce an eight-year deal, beginning September 1 and running through the 2031–32 academic year, that would see ESPN and its sister networks carry championship events in 21 women's and 19 men's sports across the three NCAA divisions, most notably continued coverage of the [[NCAA Division I women's basketball tournament|Division I women's basketball tournament]].
|<ref>{{Cite press release |title=ESPN and NCAA Reach New, Eight-Year Media Rights Agreement |date=January 4, 2024 |url=https://espnpressroom.com/us/press-releases/2024/01/espn-and-ncaa-reach-new-eight-year-media-rights-agreement |work=ESPN Press Room|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240105014652/https://espnpressroom.com/us/press-releases/2024/01/espn-and-ncaa-reach-new-eight-year-media-rights-agreement|archive-date=January 5, 2024|url-status=live}}</ref>

|-
! 5
| [[Nigel Lythgoe]] announces he has resigned from his role on ''[[So You Think You Can Dance (American TV series)|So You Think You Can Dance]]'' in the wake of two sexual misconduct lawsuits filed against him. Lythgoe, who has denied the accusations, co-created the [[Fox Broadcasting Company|Fox]] reality competition series in 2005, and had served as its executive producer and lead judge.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Knolle|first=Sharon|date=January 5, 2024 |title=Nigel Lythgoe Exits 'So You Think You Can Dance' After Sexual Assault Lawsuits |url=https://www.thewrap.com/nigel-lythgoe-so-you-think-you-can-dance-exit-sexual-assault-lawsuits |website=The Wrap|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240106003708/https://www.thewrap.com/nigel-lythgoe-so-you-think-you-can-dance-exit-sexual-assault-lawsuits/|archive-date=January 6, 2024|url-status=live}}</ref>

|-
! rowspan=2|7
| Rather than stay with the network as a commentator and fill-in anchor, [[Medhi Hasan]] concludes the last episode of his self-titled [[MSNBC]] weekend show by stating he would depart the network entirely. ''The Mehdi Hasan Show''{{'}}s cancellation was announced in November 2023 by MSNBC as part of a revamp of its weekend schedule that took effect the weekend of January 13–14.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Johnson |first=Ted |date=January 7, 2024 |title=Mehdi Hasan Says He's Leaving MSNBC After Cancellation Of His Show |url=https://deadline.com/2024/01/medhi-hasan-leaving-msnbc-1235698021 |website=[[Deadline Hollywood]]|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240108030517/https://deadline.com/2024/01/medhi-hasan-leaving-msnbc-1235698021/|archive-date=January 8, 2024|url-status=live}}</ref><ref name="MSNBCWeekendRevamp">{{Cite web |last=Johnson |first=Ted |date=November 30, 2023 |title=MSNBC To Drop Mehdi Hasan's Show, Launch ''The Weekend'' As Part Of Overhaul Of Saturday And Sunday Lineup |url=https://deadline.com/2023/11/msnbc-weekend-mehdi-hasan-1235644017 |website=[[Deadline Hollywood]]|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231130162112/https://deadline.com/2023/11/msnbc-weekend-mehdi-hasan-1235644017/|archive-date=November 30, 2023|url-status=live}}</ref>

|-
| The [[81st Golden Globe Awards]] aired on [[CBS]] and [[Paramount+]], with notable winners including Best Motion Pictures ''[[Poor Things (film)|Poor Things]]'' ([[Golden Globe Award for Best Motion Picture – Musical or Comedy|Musical or Comedy]]) and ''[[Oppenheimer (film)|Oppenheimer]]'' ([[Golden Globe Award for Best Motion Picture – Drama|Drama]], among its five wins) and TV series ''[[Succession (TV series)|Succession]]'' ([[Golden Globe Award for Best Television Series – Drama|Best Drama Series]], among four wins in a night-leading nine nominations), ''[[The Bear (TV series)|The Bear]]'' ([[Golden Globe Award for Best Television Series – Musical or Comedy|Best Musical or Comedy Series]], among its three wins), and ''[[Beef (TV series)|Beef]]'' ([[Golden Globe Award for Best Limited or Anthology Series or Television Film|Best Limited Series, Anthology, or Film]], among its three wins). The ceremony was hosted by [[Jo Koy]], whose opening monologue was poorly received by guests and critics; Koy defended himself by saying he was hired only ten days prior after several other comedians reportedly declined to host. It also marks the ceremony's return to CBS after a 42-year absence (it previously aired on the network from [[38th Golden Globe Awards|1981]] to [[39th Golden Globe Awards|1982]]); NBC had televised the Globes since 1996, aside from a one-year absence in [[79th Golden Globe Awards|2022]].
|<ref name="Golden Globes on CBS">{{Cite web |last=Andreeva |first=Nellie |date=November 17, 2023 |title=Golden Globes Land At CBS For 2024 Telecast |url=https://deadline.com/2023/11/golden-globes-cbs-2024-telecast-1235614046 |access-date=November 17, 2023 |website=Deadline Hollywood}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=January 8, 2024 |title='Oppenheimer' dominates Golden Globes, 'Poor Things' upsets 'Barbie' in comedy |url=https://apnews.com/article/2024-golden-globes-awards-show-b684b6e3d6b0ddf07b74a0370413fb7a |website=Associated Press}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite web |date=January 8, 2024 |title=The joke's on Jo Koy: Golden Globes host delivers a bad gig for the ages |url=https://www.theguardian.com/film/2024/jan/08/jo-koy-golden-globes-host-2024 |website=The Guardian}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Porter |first=Rick |date=December 21, 2023 |title=Jo Koy to Host 2024 Golden Globes |url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/news/general-news/golden-globes-2024-host-jo-koy-1235732646 |access-date=December 21, 2023 |website=The Hollywood Reporter}}</ref><br/>{{NoteTag|NBC declined to air the [[79th Golden Globe Awards]] in 2022, amid industry boycotts surrounding the [[Hollywood Foreign Press Association]]'s inaction to address the lack of diversity among the organization's members.}}

|-
! 9
| The [[Oklahoma City Thunder]] reach an agreement with [[Griffin Media]] to air eight Friday night games from its remaining [[2023–24 Oklahoma City Thunder season|2023–24 regular schedule]] sublicensed from longtime cable partner [[Bally Sports Oklahoma]] (per a clause in parent [[Diamond Sports Group]]'s 2023–24 [[National Basketball Association|NBA]] contract that allows teams to offer a selection of over-the-air telecasts to local stations within their broadcast territory). The [[regional sports network]] would produce and employ its on-air staff for the telecasts (beginning with a January 26 away game against the [[New Orleans Pelicans]]), which airs on Griffin-owned [[MyNetworkTV]] affiliate [[KSBI]]/[[Oklahoma City]] (which previously aired Thunder games from [[2008–09 Oklahoma City Thunder season|2008]] to [[2010–11 Oklahoma City Thunder season|2011]]) and local news subchannel [[KOTV-DT]]3/[[Tulsa, Oklahoma|Tulsa]].
|<ref>{{Cite news|last=Mussatto|first=Joe |date=January 9, 2024 |title=OKC Thunder, Griffin Media agree to broadcast remaining 2023–24 Friday games locally |url=https://www.oklahoman.com/story/sports/nba/thunder/2024/01/09/okc-thunder-griffin-media-broadcast-2023-24-friday-games-ksbi-news-on-6-now-bally-sports-nba/72157714007 |work=[[The Oklahoman]]|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240109200720/https://www.oklahoman.com/story/sports/nba/thunder/2024/01/09/okc-thunder-griffin-media-broadcast-2023-24-friday-games-ksbi-news-on-6-now-bally-sports-nba/72157714007/|archive-date=January 9, 2024|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |date=January 9, 2024 |title=Thunder Friday Nights |url=https://www.nba.com/thunder/watchlocal |work=Oklahoma City Thunder |publisher=[[National Basketball Association|NBA]]}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite news |last=Bracht |first=Mel |date=October 3, 2008 |title=KSBI to air Thunder games |url=https://www.oklahoman.com/article/3306605/ksbi-to-air-thunder-games |work=The Oklahoman}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |date=August 3, 2010 |title=Thunder Signs Exclusive Television Agreement with FOX Sports Southwest |url=https://www.nba.com/thunder/news/thunder_signs_tvFXSW_100803.html |work=OKCThunder.com |publisher=NBA Media Ventures, LLC}}</ref><br/>{{NoteTag|Bally Sports Oklahoma has served as the Oklahoma City Thunder's regional cable broadcaster since the [[regional sports network|network]] launched in 2008 (as the Fox Sports Southwest spinoff Fox Sports Oklahoma), following the team's [[Seattle SuperSonics relocation to Oklahoma City|relocation from Seattle]]. The ten-game package will also be regionally simulcast on stations owned by [[Gray Television]] ([[KSWO-TV|KSWO-DT3]]/[[Lawton, Oklahoma|Lawton]] and [[KSCW-DT|KSCW]]/[[Wichita, Kansas|Wichita]]) and [[Morgan Murphy Media]] ([[KOAM-TV|KOAM]]–[[KFJX]]-DT1/DT3/[[Joplin, Missouri|Joplin]]–[[Pittsburg, Kansas|Pittsburg]]).}}

! 10
| Two events featuring [[Republican Party of the United States|Republican Party]] hopefuls for [[President of the United States|president]] are concurrently telecast from [[Des Moines, Iowa]]: [[CNN]] hosts the fifth [[2024 Republican Party presidential debates and forums|GOP debate of the campaign season]] featuring [[List of governors of Florida|Florida Governor]] [[Ron DeSantis]] and former [[List of ambassadors of the United States to the United Nations|UN Ambassador]] [[Nikki Haley]], while [[Fox News]] [[Counterprogramming (television)|counterprograms]] with a town hall featuring nomination frontrunner [[Donald Trump]], the former president's first live appearance on that network since 2022.
|<ref>{{Cite web |date=January 10, 2024 |title=Haley and DeSantis tear into each other's records in a hostile head-to-head Republican debate |url=https://apnews.com/article/republican-debate-nikki-haley-ron-desantis-cfb2d8f680c0f77431f4679d6995a135 |website=Associated Press}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=January 10, 2024 |title=Donald Trump enjoys a relaxed Fox News town hall while top Republican rivals have fiery debate |url=https://apnews.com/article/fox-trump-2024-election-e0b49cf505a0cc8f8779b6d7e4cadef0 |website=Associated Press}}</ref>

|-
! 13
| The [[2023 Miami Dolphins season|Miami Dolphins]] play the [[2023 Kansas City Chiefs|Kansas City Chiefs]] in an [[National Football League|NFL]] [[2023–24 NFL playoffs|Wild Card game]]. [[Peacock (streaming service)|Peacock]] broadcasts the contest nationally, the first NFL playoff game to be carried exclusively on a [[streaming service]] (though [[NBC]]'s [[WTVJ|Miami]] and [[KSHB-TV|Kansas City]] affiliates carry it locally to satisfy the league's local broadcast requirements).
|<ref>{{Cite web |date=January 11, 2024 |title=Peacock Enters Uncharted Waters by Streaming N.F.L. Playoff Game |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2024/01/11/business/media/peacock-streaming-nfl-playoff.html |website=[[The New York Times]]}}</ref>

|-
! 14
| The [[29th Critics' Choice Awards]] were held at the [[Santa Monica Airport|Barker Hangar]] in [[Santa Monica, California]], with notable winners including films ''Oppenheimer'' and ''[[Barbie (film)|Barbie]]'' (with eight and six wins, respectively) and television programs ''[[The Bear (TV series)|The Bear]]'', ''[[Beef (TV series)|Beef]]'' (with four wins each) and ''[[Succession (TV series)|Succession]]'' (with three wins). [[Chelsea Handler]] hosted the ceremony on [[The CW]], which used the broadcast to unveil a new brand identity that drops the "The" from its redefined logo (though "The CW" remains the network's name) and replaces its long-time green signature color with a red–orange "hot sauce" palette.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Shanfeld |first=Ethan |date=January 14, 2024 |title=''Oppenheimer'' Leads Critics Choice Awards With 8 Wins, ''Barbie'' Scores 6 |url=https://variety.com/2024/film/news/critics-choice-awards-2024-winners-list-1235870581 |website=[[Variety (magazine)|Variety]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Schneider |first=Michael |date=2024-01-12 |title=The CW Unveils New Branding Look, Dropping the 'The' and Embracing a 'Hot Sauce' Color Palette (EXCLUSIVE) |url=https://variety.com/2024/tv/news/the-cw-new-branding-logo-look-critics-choice-awards-1235870260/ |access-date=2024-09-21 |website=Variety |language=en-US}}</ref>

|-
! 15
| The [[75th Primetime Emmy Awards]] aired on [[Fox Broadcasting Company|Fox]] from the [[Peacock Theater]] in Los Angeles with [[Anthony Anderson]] as host. Notable winners include ''[[The Bear (TV series)|The Bear]]'' and ''[[Succession (TV series)|Succession]]'' (both with six major wins each, including [[Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Comedy Series|Outstanding Comedy Series]] and [[Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Drama Series|Outstanding Drama Series]], respectively), and ''[[Beef (TV series)|Beef]]'' (with five awards, including [[Primetime Emmy Award for Outstanding Limited or Anthology Series|Outstanding Limited or Anthology Series]]). The ceremony was rescheduled from its original date of September 18, 2023 due to the [[2023 Hollywood labor disputes|Hollywood labor disputes]].
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Andreeva |first=Nellie |date=August 10, 2023 |title=2023 Emmys Set January Date On Fox After Delay Amid Hollywood Strikes |url=https://deadline.com/2023/08/2023-emmys-january-2024-date-martin-luther-king-jr-day-fox-delay-hollywood-strikes-1235458917 |access-date=August 10, 2023 |website=[[Deadline Hollywood]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Vlessing |first=Etan |date=December 13, 2023 |title=Anthony Anderson to Host Emmy Awards Delayed by Hollywood Strikes |url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/tv/tv-news/anthony-anderson-emmy-awards-host-1235751961 |access-date=December 13, 2023 |website=[[The Hollywood Reporter]]}}</ref>

|-
! 17
| [[Diamond Sports Group]], owner of the [[Bally Sports]] [[Regional sports network|regional networks]], announces a bankruptcy restructuring agreement (subject to court approval) that would see [[Amazon (company)|Amazon]] become a minority investor in the company (acquiring a 15% share, in exchange for a $115 million investment) and have [[Prime Video]] become its primary streaming partner. The [[Sinclair Broadcast Group]] subsidiary has been in [[Chapter 11, Title 11, United States Code|Chapter 11 bankruptcy]] since March 2023, and the deal will also see Sinclair make a $495 million cash payment to settle a lawsuit (filed in July 2023) alleging that Sinclair received about $1.5 billion in transactions that were designed to benefit itself while damaging Diamond's finances.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Szalai |first=Georg |date=January 17, 2024 |title=Amazon to Buy Minority Stake in Diamond Sports, Sinclair to Pay $495M in Settlement |url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/business/business-news/amazon-diamond-sports-investment-reorganization-sinclair-settlement-1235790585 |access-date=January 17, 2024 |website=[[Hollywood Reporter]]}}</ref>

|-
! rowspan="2"|23
| [[World Wrestling Entertainment]] and [[Netflix]] announce a ten-year, $5 billion deal to carry ''[[WWE Raw]]'' in the United States and Canada beginning in January 2025, which resulted in the end of the program's run on cable television after 31 years across two networks ([[USA Network]] from 1993 to 2000 and from 2005 to 2024, and [[Spike TV]] from 2000 to 2005). Under the same deal, Netflix eventually gained global rights to ''Raw'', and international rights to all other WWE programming, as existing contracts expire.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Sherman |first1=Alex |last2=Pramuk |first2=Jacob |date=January 24, 2024 |title=Netflix to stream WWE's Raw starting next year in its biggest jump into live entertainment |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2024/01/23/netflix-to-stream-wwes-raw-starting-next-year.html |access-date=January 24, 2024 |website=[[CNBC]] |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240123123628/https://www.cnbc.com/2024/01/23/netflix-to-stream-wwes-raw-starting-next-year.html|archive-date=January 23, 2024|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |date=January 23, 2024|title=Netflix Makes $5 Billion Deal for 'WWE Raw' in Livestreaming Push |url=https://www.wsj.com/business/media/netflix-wins-wwe-raw-rights-in-livestreaming-push-a0a73542 |access-date=January 23, 2024|url-access=subscription|work=[[The Wall Street Journal]]|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240123123843/https://www.wsj.com/business/media/netflix-wins-wwe-raw-rights-in-livestreaming-push-a0a73542|archive-date=January 23, 2024|url-status=live}}</ref>

|-
| The [[Dallas Mavericks]] reach an agreement with [[Tegna Inc.|Tegna]]-owned [[WFAA]]/[[Dallas]]–[[Fort Worth, Texas|Fort Worth]] to air ten games from its remaining [[2023–24 Dallas Mavericks season|2023–24 regular schedule]] sublicensed from [[Bally Sports Southwest]] (per a clause in Diamond Sports' NBA contract for the season, see January 9 entry), which produces and employ its on-air staff for the telecasts. The package of Bally-produced games (which began with a March 1 away game against the [[Boston Celtics]]) joins three national [[NBA on ABC|ABC]]-televised games that were already slated to air on WFAA through April (including a January 24 home game against the [[Phoenix Suns]]). The Mavericks last televised their games over-the-air on [[CBS News and Stations|CBS]]-owned [[KTXA]] from [[2000–01 Dallas Mavericks season|2000]] to [[2020–21 Dallas Mavericks season|2021]], when longtime cable home Bally Sports Southwest became their exclusive regional TV partner.
|<ref>{{Cite press release |title=TEGNA Station WFAA Teams with the Dallas Mavericks to Broadcast 10 Additional Games Free Over-The-Air |date=January 23, 2023 |url=https://www.tegna.com/tegna-station-wfaa-teams-with-the-dallas-mavericks-to-broadcast-10-additional-games-free-over-the-air |website=[[Tegna Inc.|Tegna]]|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240123192845/https://www.tegna.com/tegna-station-wfaa-teams-with-the-dallas-mavericks-to-broadcast-10-additional-games-free-over-the-air/|archive-date=January 23, 2024|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=January 23, 2024|title=WFAA to locally broadcast 10 of the Dallas Mavericks' remaining 2023–2024 NBA regular season games |url=https://www.wfaa.com/article/sports/nba/mavericks/wfaa-dallas-mavericks-local-broadcast-over-the-air-free/287-6f040cda-65f7-4318-b910-713534fec6a1 |website=[[WFAA]] |publisher=Tegna}}</ref><br/>{{NoteTag|Bally Sports Southwest has served as the Dallas Mavericks' regional cable broadcaster since the network launched in 1983 (as Home Sports Entertainment). The ten-game package will also be regionally simulcast on Gray Television-owned stations [[KXII]]-DT2/DT3/[[Sherman, Texas]]–[[Ada, Oklahoma]] and [[KLCW-TV|KLCW]]–[[KMYL-LD|KMYL]]/[[Lubbock, Texas|Lubbock]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Tom Butts |date=March 1, 2024 |title=Gray to Broadcast 10 Dallas Mavericks Games in March & April |url=https://www.tvtechnology.com/news/gray-to-broadcast-10-dallas-mavericks-games-in-march-and-april |access-date=March 5, 2024 |website=TVTechnology |publisher=Future US, Inc.}}</ref>}}

|-
! 24
| [[Comedy Central]] announces that [[Jon Stewart]] would return to ''[[The Daily Show]]'' as an executive producer and through the [[2024 United States elections|2024 U.S. election]] cycle, host on Monday nights (beginning February 12), with members of the show's correspondents roster anchoring the rest of the week. Stewart had served as the show's host from 1999 to 2015.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Rose |first=Lacey |date=January 24, 2024 |title=Jon Stewart Returns to 'The Daily Show' as Host |url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/tv/tv-news/jon-stewart-the-daily-show-host-1235802984 |access-date=January 24, 2024 |website=The Hollywood Reporter}}</ref>

|-
! 28
| The [[Milwaukee Bucks]] reach an agreement with [[Weigel Broadcasting]] to air a package of ten games from its remaining [[2023–24 Milwaukee Bucks season|2023–24 regular schedule]] on [[independent station|independent]] [[WMLW-TV|WMLW]], along with regional simulcasts on stations throughout [[Wisconsin]] and in the [[Quad Cities]], to be produced by [[Bally Sports Wisconsin]] under a sublicensing agreement. Two of the scheduled games were simulcast on WMLW's [[Milwaukee]]-area sister stations: a February 23 away game against the [[Minnesota Timberwolves]] (the first of the slate) that also airs on co-owned [[CBS]] affiliate [[WDJT-TV|WDJT]], and a March 4 home game against the [[Los Angeles Clippers]] that will be presented in Spanish on [[Telemundo]] affiliate [[WYTU-LD|WYTU]]. They are the first local over-the-air Bucks telecasts since the team's [[1988–89 Milwaukee Bucks season|19-year]] relationship with [[WCGV-TV|WCGV]] ended in [[2006–07 Milwaukee Bucks season|2007]].
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Prosser, Emmett |date=January 28, 2024 |title=Local station WMLW to televise 10 Milwaukee Bucks games starting in February |url=https://www.jsonline.com/story/sports/nba/bucks/2024/01/28/local-station-wmlw-to-televise-10-milwaukee-bucks-games/72389586007 |website=Milwaukee Journal-Sentinel}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=January 28, 2024 |title=Weigel's WMLW, WDJT & Telemundo Wisconsin To Bring Milwaukee Bucks Games Back To Free Over-The-Air TV |url=https://tvnewscheck.com/programming/article/weigels-wmlw-wdjt-telemundo-wisconsin-to-bring-milwaukee-bucks-games-back-to-free-over-the-air-tv |access-date=January 29, 2024 |website=TVNewsCheck }}</ref><br/>{{NoteTag|Bally Sports Wisconsin has served as the Milwaukee Bucks' regional cable broadcaster since the network launched in 2007 as Fox Sports Wisconsin, a spinoff of [[Bally Sports North|Fox Sports North]] (which itself had held the team's cable rights since that network, then known as Midwest Sports Channel, merged with the [[Wisconsin Sports Network]] in 1996. The ten-game package will also be regionally simulcast on stations owned by Morgan Murphy Media ([[WISC-TV|WISC-DT1/DT3]]/[[Madison, Wisconsin|Madison]]), Gray Television ([[WBAY-TV|WBAY-DT1/DT3]])/[[Green Bay, Wisconsin|Green Bay]], [[WEAU]]–WECX/[[Eau Claire, Wisconsin|Eau Claire]]–[[La Crosse, Wisconsin|La Crosse]], and [[WSAW-TV|WSAW]]–[[WAOW#WYOW|WYOW]]/[[Wausau, Wisconsin|Wausau]]), and Tegna ([[WQAD-TV|WQAD-DT3]]/[[Davenport, Iowa]]).<ref>{{Cite web |date=February 9, 2024 |title=Weigel & Morgan Murphy's WISC To Bring Milwaukee Bucks Games Back To Free Over-The-Air TV In Madison |url=https://tvnewscheck.com/programming/article/weigel-and-morgan-murphys-wisc-to-bring-milwaukee-bucks-games-back-to-free-over-the-air-tv-in-madison |website=TVNewsCheck }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=February 8, 2024 |title=Weigel and Tegna's WQAD To Bring Milwaukee Bucks Games Back To Free Over-The-Air TV In The Quad Cities |url=https://tvnewscheck.com/programming/article/weigel-and-tegnas-wqad-to-bring-milwaukee-bucks-games-back-to-free-over-the-air-tv-in-the-quad-cities |access-date=February 9, 2024 |website=TVNewsCheck }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |date=February 9, 2024 |title=WBAY to broadcast Milwaukee Bucks games |url=https://www.wbay.com/2024/02/09/wbay-broadcast-milwaukee-bucks-games |work=[[WBAY-TV]] |publisher=[[Gray Television]]}}</ref>}}

=== February ===
{| class="wikitable sortable"
! Date
! Event
! Ref.

|-
! 1
| [[Gray Television]] announces it reached an agreement to trade CBS/[[The CW Plus|CW+]] affiliate [[KGWN]]/[[Cheyenne, Wyoming|Cheyenne]], [[NBC]]/CW+ affiliate [[KCWY]]/[[Casper, Wyoming]], and NBC affiliate [[KNEP]]/[[Scottsbluff, Nebraska]] (which concurrently shut down its news bureau, replaced in-house newscasts with simulcasts from KGWN/KCWY's Cheyenne-based ''Wyoming News Now'' operation, and laid off most of its Scottsbluff-based staff) to [[Marquee Broadcasting]], in exchange for the latter group's [[Planning permission#Broadcasting|construction permit]] for the proposed KCBU/[[Salt Lake City]]. Neither company will receive additional cash or other compensation as part of the transaction.
|<ref>{{Cite web |date=February 1, 2024 |title=Gray and Marquee Broadcasting Swap Television Station Assets |url=https://graytv.gcs-web.com/node/21466/pdf |website=[[Gray Television]] |format=PDF}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Lafayette |first=Jon |date=February 1, 2024 |title=Gray Trades With Marquee Broadcasting for Permit To Build Salt Lake Station |url=https://www.nexttv.com/news/gray-trades-with-marquee-broadcasting-for-permit-to-build-salt-lake-station |website=[[Broadcasting & Cable]] |publisher=Future US Inc.}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite web |date=February 5, 2024 |title=Gray Goes Black on KNEP; Shuts Down Scottsbluff TV Station |url=https://ruralradio.com/khyy/news/gray-goes-black-on-knep-shuts-down-scottsbluff-tv-station |access-date=February 7, 2024 |website=[[Rural Radio Network]]}}</ref><br/>{{NoteTag|The [[KCBU]] call letters were previously assigned to a television station licensed to [[Price, Utah]], which operated from October 2003 to June 2009. [[Equity Media Holdings]] sold the station at auction to religious broadcaster [[Daystar Television Network|Daystar]] in April 2009, three months before the station ceased [[Digital television transition in the United States|analog operations]]. (KCBU's digital transmission facilities were never built.) Daystar cancelled the station's FCC license in July 2010, refocusing on returning sister [[KUTF]]/[[Logan, Utah|Logan]] to the air instead.}}

|-
! 2
| Twelve [[Cox Media Group]]-owned stations in nine markets are removed from [[DirecTV]] and co-owned [[DirecTV Stream]] and [[U-verse TV|U-verse]] in a [[carriage dispute|contract renewal impasse]], resulting in the complete loss of satellite distribution for the group's stations. (Cox has been in a dispute with Dish Network since November 2022, resulting in the removal of those 12 stations, out of 13 operated by the group.) The Cox-owned stations were restored on February 11, in turn, averting subscriber blackouts of CBS's [[Super Bowl LVIII]] coverage in the [[KIRO-TV|Seattle]] and [[WHIO-TV|Dayton]] markets.

| Actress [[Gina Carano]] sues [[The Walt Disney Company]] and co-owned [[Lucasfilm]] on claims she was [[wrongful dismissal|wrongfully fired]] from ''[[The Mandalorian]]'', seeking an order forcing the studio to rehire her or pay at least $75,000 in damages. Carano, who played [[Cara Dune]] during the [[Disney+]] [[space Western]]'s first two seasons, was fired in February 2021 after comparing the treatment of [[Conservatism in the United States|conservatives]] to the [[The Holocaust in Germany|Nazi persecution of Jews]] in an [[Instagram]] post (one of several inflammatory social media posts of hers that were heavily criticized for their [[Gina Carano#Political views|far-right viewpoints]] including remarks mocking [[Face masks during the COVID-19 pandemic in the United States|COVID-19 masking mandates]] and the inclusion of [[Personal pronoun#Gender|gender pronouns]] in social media profiles, and [[Attempts to overturn the 2020 United States presidential election|falsely claiming voter fraud]] in the [[2020 United States presidential election|2020 election]]). Carano, who received assistance from [[Twitter|X/Twitter]] parent [[X Corp.]] to cover legal fees, claims Lucasfilm did not hold male co-stars to similar standards for controversial posts aimed at Republicans (including one by series lead [[Pedro Pascal]] from 2017, comparing then-[[President of the United States|President]] [[Donald Trump]] to [[Adolf Hitler|Hitler]]), and subjected her to harassment and hurt her future work prospects.

| [[ESPN Inc.]], [[Fox Corporation]] and [[Warner Bros. Discovery]] announce plans to launch a sports-focused streaming service, later branded as [[Venu Sports]] in May, that would feature live feeds from their respective linear sports and broadcast networks (including ESPN, ABC and the former's sister networks such as [[ESPN2]] and [[SEC Network]]; Fox and sister networks [[Fox Sports 1|FS1]], [[Fox Sports 2|FS2]] and [[Big Ten Network]]; and WBD-owned [[TNT (American TV network)|TNT]], [[TBS (American TV network)|TBS]] and [[truTV]]), and streaming content from [[ESPN+]], the major sports leagues (including the NBA, NHL, NFL and [[Major League Baseball]]) and college sports conferences. The service, which would also be optionally bundled with [[Disney+]], [[Hulu]], or [[Max (streaming service)|Max]], would reportedly not impact ESPN's own plans to launch a full direct-to-consumer streaming service, which the company confirmed the following day would launch in the Fall of 2025. In response, [[FuboTV]] filed an antitrust lawsuit on February 20, claiming Venu would give the three broadcasters an unfair advantage over providers such as Fubo who are forced to carry less-desirable programming for access to sports. On August 16, a federal judge issued a preliminary injunction to block Venu's launch. On September 12, the lawsuit's trial date was set for October 2025.

| [[Sam Waterston]] makes his final appearance on ''[[Law & Order]]'' as [[Jack McCoy]], as the character resigns as New York City District Attorney rather than face the political wrath of the mayor (for subpoenaing the mayor's son in a murder case) in the episode "Last Dance." Waterston, who announced his departure from the series on February 2 (after appearing in over 400 episodes in 19 seasons across 30 years), would be succeeded by [[Tony Goldwyn]] as new DA Nicholas Baxter later in [[Law & Order (season 23)|the current 23rd season]].
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Hatchett |first=Keisha |date=2024-02-23 |title=Law & Order's Sam Waterston Takes His Final Case as Jack McCoy in a Gripping Hour — Here's How He Left |url=https://www.tvline.com/recaps/law-and-order-recap-sam-waterston-final-epsiode-jack-mccoy-leaves-1235172684/ |access-date=2024-09-21 |website=TVLine |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Cordero |first=Rosy |date=February 2, 2024 |title=Sam Waterston Exiting 'Law & Order' As Tony Goldwyn Joins Cast |url=https://deadline.com/2024/02/sam-waterston-exit-law-and-order-tony-goldwyn-cast-1235812525 |access-date=February 2, 2024 |website=Deadline Hollywood}}</ref>

! rowspan="2"|28
| [[Tom Skilling]] retires from [[WGN-TV]]/[[Chicago]], after nearly 46 years as meteorologist for the [[Nexstar Media Group|Nexstar]]-owned independent station. His final evening newscasts that day capped off a month-long tribute to Skilling, who received reverence in the Chicago broadcasting and national meteorological communities during his tenure (the latter resulting from his weathercasts being televised over WGN's former [[WGN America|superstation feed]] (now [[NewsNation]]) from 1978 to 2014) for the in-depth analysis and striking accuracy of his forecasts. Demetrius Ivory, who joined the station in 2013, succeeded Skilling as chief meteorologist on February 29.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Di Nunzio, Miriam |date=February 28, 2024 |title=Tom Skilling's final forecast — longtime WGN-TV meteorologist signs off amid laughter and tears |url=https://chicago.suntimes.com/news/2024/02/29/tom-skilling-final-forecast-longtime-wgn-tv-meteorologist-signs-off-amid-laughter-and-tears |website=Chicago Sun-Times}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Diana Dionisio |date=February 1, 2024 |title=WGN-TV names Demetrius Ivory as chief meteorologist, as legendary, longtime Chief Meteorologist Tom Skilling retires |url=https://wgntv.com/community/news-releases/wgn-tv-names-demetrius-ivory-as-chief-meteorologist-as-legendary-longtime-chief-meteorologist-tom-skilling-retires |access-date=March 2, 2024 |work=[[WGN-TV]] |publisher=[[Nexstar Media Group]]}}</ref>

|-
| Nexstar Media Group petitions the [[U.S. Court of Appeals for the Fifth Circuit|Fifth Circuit Court of Appeals]] to review changes to FCC broadcast ownership rules passed in December 2023 that closed loopholes allowing licensees to hold more than one [[Big Three television networks|Big Four]] affiliation in a [[media market|market]] by placing certain networks on [[digital subchannel|subchannels]] or [[low-power broadcasting|low-power stations]], and upheld a 1996 rule prohibiting broadcasters from owning stations that reach more than 39% of U.S. households in total. Nexstar asserts that the FCC overstepped its authority by tightening ownership rules despite competition to broadcast media by pay television and streaming, and claims the order had numerous procedural problems and faulty conclusions in violation of the [[Administrative Procedure Act]] and the [[Telecommunications Act of 1996|1996 Telecommunications Act]].
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Ted Hearn |date=February 29, 2024 |title=Nexstar Sues FCC Over Media Ownership Rules |url=https://policyband.com/blog/nexstar-sues-fcc-over-media-ownership-rules |access-date=March 6, 2024 |website=PolicyBand}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Matthew Keys |date=February 29, 2024 |title=Nexstar asks court to review FCC ownership limits |url=https://thedesk.net/news/nexstar-sues-fcc-quadrennial-review-media-ownership |access-date=March 6, 2024 |website=TheDesk |publisher=Solano Media, LLC}}</ref><br/>{{NoteTag|The FCC typically reviews its media ownership regulations every four years; however, administrative delays to its 2018 quadrennial review effort prompted a September 2023 lawsuit by the [[National Association of Broadcasters]] to force the agency—which was given a 90-day notice to issue its changes—to finalize the review. Nexstar Media Group has long employed [[local marketing agreement|outsourcing agreements]] with various third-party licensees to control stations it cannot own directly under FCC rules; in addition to owning 197 stations outright as of 2024, Nexstar controls 40 additional stations owned by third parties (such as [[Mission Broadcasting]], [[Vaughan Media]] and [[White Knight Broadcasting]]) under such agreements.}}

! 7
| [[President of the United States|President]] [[Joe Biden]] delivers his third [[2024 State of the Union Address|State of the Union address]], airing along with associated coverage on all major broadcast and cable news networks. In the speech, Biden addressed issues such as the economy, democracy, [[United States abortion-rights movement|abortion rights]] (directly addressing [[Supreme Court of the United States|Supreme Court]] justices in an unscripted moment regarding the [[Dobbs v. Jackson Women's Health Organization|2022 court decision]] that overturned ''[[Roe v. Wade]]''), [[Mexico–United States border crisis|immigration]] (reiterating calls for House Republicans to pass bipartisan immigration legislation stalled under pressure from Republican presidential candidate [[Donald Trump]] and mentioning the February [[murder of Laken Riley]] in the context of border policy), the [[Russian invasion of Ukraine]], and the [[Gaza war]] (advocating for a temporary ceasefire and [[two-state solution]] to the [[Israeli–Palestinian conflict|conflict]], and demanding [[Prime Minister of Israel|Israeli Prime Minister]] [[Benjamin Netanyahu]] allow in humanitarian aid to [[Gaza Strip|Gaza]] civilians). The [[Republican Party (United States)|Republican]] response delivered by Alabama U.S. Senator [[Katie Britt]], which primarily touched on immigration, drew largely negative reviews (including from Republicans), and was criticized for its inaccuracy (including a misrepresentation of the story of Karla Jacinto Romero, who was sold into sex trafficking while in [[Mexico]] during [[George W. Bush]]'s presidency) and for Britt's stylistic delivery (described as "dramatic", "creepy", "insincere" and "over-the-top", and questioning her decision to conduct the response in her kitchen). The address drew 32.2 million viewers across nine networks, with Fox News registering the highest viewership at 5.640 million.

! 10
| The [[96th Academy Awards]] air on [[American Broadcasting Company|ABC]] from the [[Dolby Theatre]], with [[Jimmy Kimmel]] as host. ''[[Oppenheimer (film)|Oppenheimer]]'' led all nominees in overall wins with seven awards, including [[Academy Award for Best Picture|Best Picture]], [[Academy Award for Best Actor|Best Actor]] ([[Cillian Murphy]]), [[Academy Award for Best Supporting Actor|Best Supporting Actor]] ([[Robert Downey Jr.]]) and [[Academy Award for Best Director|Best Director]] ([[Christopher Nolan]]). Other major winners included ''[[Poor Things (film)|Poor Things]]'' with four awards, including [[Academy Award for Best Actor|Best Actress]] ([[Emma Stone]]); and ''[[The Zone of Interest (film)|The Zone of Interest]]'' with two, including [[Academy Award for Best International Feature Film|Best International Feature Film]]. [[Billie Eilish]] and [[Finneas O'Connell|Finneas]] won [[Academy Award for Best Original Song|Best Original Song]] (for "[[What Was I Made For?]]" from the ''[[Barbie (film)|Barbie]]'' [[Barbie the Album|film soundtrack]]), becoming the youngest two-time Oscar winners in history (at ages 22 and 26, respectively), having previously won the award in [[94th Academy Awards|2022]]. The telecast was watched by 19.5 million viewers, the highest viewership for any awards show since 2020.

! 14
| In a 3–2 party-line vote (backed solely by its Democratic commissioners), the Federal Communications Commission, as part of a broader effort by the [[Presidency of Joe Biden|Biden administration]] to curb [[Fee#Hidden fees|hidden "junk" fees]] charged to customers, passes new rules requiring cable and satellite providers to disclose the total cost of their programming packages (including any additional programming-related fees, such as surcharges for receiving local stations and regional sports networks) in billing and promotional materials. The [[NCTA (association)|NCTA]] asserts that the rules, as written, would create confusion for customers and that providers already offer "clear and accurate pricing information" about their programming tiers.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Ted Johnson |date=March 14, 2024 |title=Cable And Satellite Providers Required To Disclose "All In" Pricing Under Newly Passed FCC Rules |url=https://deadline.com/2024/03/fcc-cable-and-satellite-prices-1235858769 |website=Deadline Hollywood |publisher=Penske Media Corporation}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Julia Shapero |date=March 15, 2024 |title=FCC cracking down on cable 'junk fees' |url=https://thehill.com/policy/technology/4534536-fcc-cracking-down-on-cable-junk-fees |website=The Hill |publisher=Nexstar Media Group}}</ref>

! 17
| Three days after the FCC voted to require television providers to disclose "all-in" pricing (incorporating surcharges for receiving certain channels) and likely in response to recent carriage disputes with station operators, [[DirecTV]] introduces a "No Locals" option for its Entertainment, Choice, Ultimate and Premier programming tiers, allowing its satellite customers the ability to opt out of receiving local broadcast stations available in their home markets for a $12 monthly discount. (It joins rival satellite provider [[Dish Network]], which has offered such opt-outs since 2017, in allowing customers to exclude local stations from their channel packages.)
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Jon Lafayette |date=March 17, 2024 |title=DirecTV to Let Satellite Customers Drop Local Stations From Bundle to Cut Bill |url=https://www.nexttv.com/news/directv-drops-local-stations-from-lower-priced-bundle |publisher=Future US, Inc. |magazine=Broadcasting & Cable}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Jon Lafayette |date=March 19, 2024 |title=No Local Stations? Dish Says It's Been Offering Opt-Outs for 7 Years |url=https://www.nexttv.com/news/no-local-stations-dish-says-its-been-offering-opt-outs-for-7-years |website=NextTV |publisher=Future US, Inc.}}</ref><br/>{{NoteTag|Customers could still receive local stations for free using an optional antenna.}}

! 18
| Then-former [[President of the United States|U.S. president]] and 2024 presumptive Republican presidential nominee Donald Trump sues [[ABC News (United States)|ABC News]] and [[George Stephanopoulos]] for [[defamation]], claiming the ''[[This Week (American TV program)|This Week]]'' moderator harmed his reputation by stating multiple times in a March 10 interview with [[United States House of Representatives|Representative]] [[Nancy Mace]] (R-[[South Carolina|S.C.]]) that Trump had been found civilly liable for [[rape|raping]] former ''[[Vogue (magazine)|Vogue]]'' advice columnist [[E. Jean Carroll]]. The lawsuit, filed in the [[United States District Court for the Southern District of Florida|U.S. District Court for the Southern District of Florida]], stems from Stephanopoulos' questioning of Mace, a rape survivor, about her continued support of Trump despite two recent jury verdicts in related [[E. Jean Carroll v. Donald J. Trump|civil battery and defamation suits]] that found he sexually abused Carroll, who has been awarded $88.3 million in damages, in 1995/96. (Mace accused Stephanopoulos of trying to “shame” her by probing why she endorsed Trump for president.) While the jury in the initial 2023 civil defamation case technically found Trump liable for [[sexual assault|sexually assaulting]] and defaming Carroll, Judge [[Lewis A. Kaplan|Lewis Kaplan]] later clarified that they found that Trump raped her by forcible [[Fingering (sexual act)|digital penetration]] (defined as sexual assault under New York's penal code), concluding her allegation of rape was “substantially true" in the broader context beyond the state's legal definition. ABC later settled the lawsuit for $15 million on December 14 of the same year.
|<ref>{{Cite news |last=Oliver Darcy |date=March 19, 2024 |title=Trump sues ABC News and George Stephanopoulos for defamation over Nancy Mace interview |url=https://www.cnn.com/2024/03/19/media/trump-sues-abc-news-george-stephanopoulos/index.html |work=CNN }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last1=Michael M. Grynbaum |last2=Jim Rutenberg |date=March 18, 2023 |title=Trump Sues ABC and Stephanopoulos, Saying They Defamed Him |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2024/03/18/us/politics/trump-lawsuit-abc-stephanopoulos.html |work=The New York Times}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite news |last=Sudiksha Kochi |date=March 11, 2024 |title=Nancy Mace accuses ABC host of trying to 'shame' her for supporting Donald Trump as a rape survivor |url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/news/politics/2024/03/11/mace-stephanopoulos-shaming-trump/72928953007 |work=[[USA Today]] }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last1=Reid |first1=Paula |last2=Polantz |first2=Katelyn |date=2024-12-14 |title=ABC News settles defamation suit with Trump for $15 million |url=https://www.cnn.com/2024/12/14/politics/trump-abc-news-defamation-lawsuit-settle/index.html |access-date=2024-12-15 |website=CNN |language=en}}</ref>

| [[Bellator MMA]] announces a distribution deal with [[Warner Bros. Discovery]] that would see [[Max (streaming service)|Max]] acquire streaming rights to the promotion's [[mixed martial arts]] events (including its eight 2024 Champions Series fights, starting with [[Bellator 302]] on March 22), archival fight cards (dating to Bellator's 2008 inception) and original docuseries, along with additional fights and ancillary content on TruTV. The WBD deal replaces a previous agreement with former owner [[Paramount Global]], which acquired the promotion in 2011 (as [[Viacom (2005–2019)|Viacom]]) and sold it to the [[Professional Fighters League]] in 2023. (Outside of a 2019–20 deal with [[DAZN]], Bellator matches aired on multiple Paramount/Viacom-owned networks during that timeframe, last airing on [[Showtime (TV network)|Showtime]] from 2021 to 2023, with that deal ending as a result of Paramount shuttering the premium channel's sports division.)

! 20
| The [[College Football Playoff]] announces a six-year deal with [[ESPN]] (worth an estimated $7.8 billion overall) that would see the network retain broadcast rights to the [[College Football Playoff]] through the 2020–31 academic year, and would transfer the [[College Football Playoff National Championship|CFP National Championship]] game from ESPN to sister network ABC (which carried the predecessor [[BCS National Championship Game]] from 1999 to 2006) starting in 2026. The agreement also modifies terms of the remaining two years of ESPN's initial contract for the CFP television rights (a 12-year deal that was signed in 2014) to account for the tournament's expansion from four to 12 teams, starting with the 2024–25 college football season, and allows for ESPN to sublicense a select number of CFP games to other networks.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Ralph D. Russo |date=March 20, 2024 |title=ESPN Will Remain The Home Of College Football Playoff Through 2031 Under $7.8 Billion Deal |url=https://tvnewscheck.com/uncategorized/article/espn-will-remain-the-home-of-college-football-playoff-through-2031-under-7-8-billion-deal |access-date=March 21, 2024 |website=TVNewsCheck }}</ref><ref>{{Cite press release |title=ESPN and the College Football Playoff Extend Exclusive Media Rights Agreement Through 2031–32 Season |date=March 19, 2024 |publisher=[[ESPN Inc.]] |url=https://espnpressroom.com/us/press-releases/2024/03/espn-and-the-college-football-playoff-extend-exclusive-media-rights-agreement-through-2031-32-season |access-date=March 21, 2024 |website=ESPN PressRoom}}</ref>

! 21
| The FCC fines Nexstar Media Group and partner company Mission Broadcasting a combined $1.82 million, and orders the latter to sell [[WPIX]]/[[New York City]], ruling that Nexstar unlawfully circumvented national broadcast ownership limits through a [[local marketing agreement]] it signed in December 2020, as part of an option that Mission exercised to buy the CW affiliate from the [[E. W. Scripps Company]], that resulted in "an unauthorized transfer of control" of WPIX to Nexstar through certain attributable interests. (The FCC limits a single company from owning television stations that reach a combined 39% of American TV households, thus requiring Nexstar, which maintains similar operational arrangements with Mission in 26 other markets, to sell other stations in its portfolio in order to acquire WPIX while complying with the cap). Its first sale proposal to meet the cap would be disclosed on April 12, as it proposed to sell MyNetworkTV affiliate [[WJMN-TV|WJMN]]/[[Escanaba, Michigan]] (a former semi-satellite of co-owned [[WFRV-TV|WFRV]]/[[Green Bay, Wisconsin|Green Bay]] that disaffected with CBS in 2022 in a chain-wide affiliation renewal dispute with the network) to a former [[Quincy Media]] executive who would enter into an LMA with [[Morgan Murphy Media]], the new owners of ABC/CW affiliate [[WBUP]]/[[WBKP]] in the same market.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Jon Lafayette |date=March 21, 2024 |title=FCC Orders Mission To Sell WPIX New York, Fines Nexstar $1.2 Million |url=https://www.nexttv.com/news/fcc-orders-mission-to-sell-wpix-fines-nexstar-dollar12-million |publisher=Future US, Inc. |magazine=Broadcasting & Cable}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Mark K. Miller |date=July 13, 2020 |title=Mission Broadcasting Buying WPIX From Scripps |url=https://tvnewscheck.com/article/top-news/251137/mission-broadcasting-buying-wpix-from-scripps |access-date=March 21, 2024 |website=TVNewsCheck }}</ref><br/><ref name="wjmnsold">{{Cite web |date=April 10, 2024 |title=Assignments |url=https://enterpriseefiling.fcc.gov/dataentry/views/public/assignmentDraftCopy?displayType=html&appKey=25076ff38e9afacf018e9b0b32ba004f&id=25076ff38e9afacf018e9b0b32ba004f&goBack=N |access-date=April 10, 2024 |website=Licensing and Management System |publisher=[[Federal Communications Commission]]}}</ref><br/>{{NoteTag|The FCC respectively levied fines of $1.2 million against Nexstar Media Group and $620,000 against Mission Broadcasting for the violations.}}

! 22
| [[NBC News]] announces that [[Ronna McDaniel]] would join the network as a contributor for its political and Election Night coverage. The hiring of McDaniel (who, on March 8, ended her seven-year term as [[Republican National Committee]] chair, a role in which she frequently echoed Donald Trump's [[Republican reactions to Donald Trump's claims of 2020 election fraud|false claims]] that the [[2020 United States presidential election|2020 presidential election]] was rigged in Joe Biden's favor and helped organize [[Trump fake electors plot|fake electors]] in key battleground states in an attempt to [[Attempts to overturn the 2020 United States presidential election|overturn the outcome]])was met with furor within the news division including on-air denouncements from former Meet the Press moderator [[Chuck Todd]] and various [[MSNBC]] hosts and analysts. MSNBC president [[Rashida Jones (television executive)|Rashida Jones]], responding to staff backlash, announced on March 23 that McDaniel would not appear on its programs. McDaniel, for her part, used a March 24 appearance on NBC's ''[[Meet the Press]]'' with [[Kristen Welker]] (which was scheduled and recorded before her hiring by the network) to state that Biden won fairly and disagrees with Trump's desire to pardon the [[January 6 United States Capitol attack|January 6]] rioters. NBC parted ways with McDaniel on March 26, citing a lack of consensus in support of her hiring.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Johnson, Ted |date=March 22, 2024 |title=Ronna McDaniel, Former RNC Chair, Joins NBC News As Political Analyst |url=https://deadline.com/2024/03/ronna-mcdaniel-hired-by-nbc-news-commentator-1235865745 |website=Deadline}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Bauder |first=David |date=2024-03-24 |title=A vocal revolt: MSNBC personalities object to NBC News' hiring of Ronna McDaniel as a contributor |url=https://apnews.com/article/nbc-news-mcdaniel-todd-election-fraud-683aa560f0824725023dacde0f504ad6 |access-date=2024-10-01 |website=[[Associated Press]] |language=en}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite web |last=Johnson |first=Ted |date=2024-03-24 |title=Chuck Todd Chides NBC News Over Handling Of Ronna McDaniel Hire; Network Gets Backlash Over Decision To Retain Former RNC Chair As Analyst |url=https://deadline.com/2024/03/nbc-news-ronna-mcdaniel-paid-contributor-1235866884/ |access-date=2024-10-01 |website=Deadline |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Alex Weprin |date=March 22, 2024 |title=NBC News Hires Former RNC Chair Ronna McDaniel |url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/news/politics-news/nbc-news-hires-ronna-mcdaniel-msnbc-analyst-1235858150 |access-date=March 26, 2024  |magazine=The Hollywood Reporter}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite news |last=Michael M. ! 25
| CBS announces a linear–streaming rights deal with [[Dick Clark Productions]] that extends its contract for the [[Golden Globe Awards]] (initially a one-year deal for [[81st Golden Globe Awards|the 2024 edition]]) through 2030, and grants it telecast rights to the [[American Music Awards]] for an unspecified term. (Both award shows are also streamed live on [[Paramount+]].) The deal ends the AMAs' 51-year broadcast relationship with ABC, for which [[Dick Clark|Clark]] created the ceremony in 1973 following the expiration of the network's deal to televise the [[Grammy Awards]], which moved to longtime broadcaster CBS that same year. (The AMAs were last awarded in [[American Music Awards of 2022|2022]].)

! 28
| The [[Detroit Pistons]] reach an agreement with the E. W. Scripps Company to air a package of five games (out of the team's eight remaining regular season games scheduled for April) from its [[2023–24 Detroit Pistons season|2023–24 schedule]] locally on CW affiliate [[WMYD-TV|WMYD]]. The package, which would be produced by longtime cable carrier [[Bally Sports Detroit]] (which simulcast the games outside of the Detroit market) under a sublicensing agreement with Scripps Sports, begins with an April 1 home game against the [[Memphis Grizzlies]]. It marks the first time that Pistons games have been televised over-the-air since their previous [[2004–05 Detroit Pistons season|four-year]] relationship with WMYD and [[Graham Media Group|Graham]]-owned NBC affiliate [[WDIV-TV|WDIV]] ended in [[2007–08 Detroit Pistons season|2008]], in favor of an exclusive deal with the network then known as Fox Sports Detroit.

The simulcast arrangement with Bally Sports Detroit differs from other recent NBA sublicensing deals forged by parent Diamond Sports Group during the [[2023–24 NBA season]] that saw over-the-air games simulcast on several stations within the team's territory to supplement arrangements with stations in each team's home market; incidentally, in addition to WMYD and ABC-affiliated sister [[WXYZ-TV|WXYZ]] in the Detroit market, Scripps also owns stations in two nearby markets, [[WXMI]]/[[Grand Rapids, Michigan]] (Fox) and [[WSYM-TV|WSYM]]/[[Lansing, Michigan|Lansing]] (Fox/MyNetworkTV).}}

! 1–7
| The final week of the [[2024 NCAA Division I women's basketball tournament]] sees three viewership records for women's basketball being set: On April 1, [[ESPN]]'s broadcast of a regional final between [[2023-24 Iowa Hawkeyes women's basketball team|Iowa Hawkeyes]] over the [[2023–24 LSU Tigers women's basketball team|LSU Tigers]] (a 94–87 win by the Hawkeyes propelled by a 41-point performance from Hawkeye star [[Caitlin Clark]]) attracts 12.3 million viewers, surpassing the 9.9 million who watched those two schools play in the [[2023 NCAA Division I women's basketball championship game|2023 national championship]]. On April 5, a 71–69 national semifinal win by the Hawkeyes over [[2023–24 UConn Huskies women's basketball team|UConn]] attracts 14.2 million to ESPN, a record for any basketball game (college or pro, men or women) on the network. Then on April 7, the tournament's [[2024 NCAA Division I women's basketball championship game|title game]], an 87–75 win by undefeated [[2023–24 South Carolina Gamecocks women's basketball team|South Carolina]] over the Hawkeyes, attracts 18.9 million total viewers on [[American Broadcasting Company|ABC]] and ESPN, (the latter channel carried a [[Sue Bird]]/[[Diana Taurasi]]-hosted alternate telecast) it was the most-watched sporting event, outside of football and the Olympics, since 2019.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Rosenstein, Greg |date=April 6, 2024 |title=Iowa-UConn Final Four matchup draws 14.2 million viewers, most in women's college basketball history |url=https://www.nbcnews.com/news/sports/iowa-uconn-final-four-matchup-draws-142-million-viewers-womens-college-rcna146735 |website=NBC News}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=ESPN News Services |date=April 3, 2024 |title=Iowa-LSU sets women's NCAA hoops ratings record with 12.3M viewers |url=https://www.espn.com.au/womens-college-basketball/story/_/id/39862514/iowa-lsu-rematch-shatters-tv-ratings-record-123m-viewers |access-date=April 3, 2024 |website=[[ESPN.com]]}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite web |last=Campione, Katie |date=April 8, 2024 |title=South Carolina Takes 2024 NCAA Title In Most-Watched Women's College Basketball Game On Record |url=https://deadline.com/2024/04/ncaa-womens-college-basketball-championship-record-viewership-south-carolina-iowa-1235878777 |website=Deadline}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Lucia |first=Joe |date=2024-04-08 |title=South Carolina-Iowa averages 18.7 million viewers, most-watched basketball game since 2019 |url=https://awfulannouncing.com/college-basketball/south-carolina-iowa-march-madness-record-viewership.html |access-date=2024-10-01 |website=Awful Announcing |language=en-US}}</ref>

! 29
| [[WBKB-TV]], the only commercial broadcast station in [[Alpena, Michigan]] (the nation's third-smallest TV market) and carrier of four of the five major commercial television networks, begins a news partnership with Sinclair's NBC affiliate, [[WPBN-TV]]/WTOM-TV in [[Traverse City, Michigan]], to feature a full complement of newscasts and weather coverage blended with WBKB-TV's existing news operation, which becomes a ''de facto'' bureau for WPBN/WTOM, known on-air as ''UpNorthLive News''. WBKB, which had been acquired by [[Morgan Murphy Media]] in late 2023 after the death of its [[The Marks Group|former owner]] in May 2022, had struggled to maintain a full-time news and weather staff because of its market position and ownership in an era of media consolidation, mainly featuring a team staffed by recent college graduates, and also had issues maintaining full-time weather coverage, contracting short-term with freelance meteorologists and [[NewsNet]] to provide forecasts. WBKB still produces some newscasts, with its NBC subchannel carrying all ''UpNorthLive'' newscasts, and its CBS and ABC channels carrying other simulcasts depending on their own schedules. WBKB's Fox schedule continues to feature no news programming.
|<ref name="up-north-live-pr">{{Cite press release |title=WBKB expands local newscasts, partners with UpNorthLive news |date=2024-04-04 |publisher=Morgan Murphy Media |url=https://www.wbkb11.com/wbkb-expands-local-newscasts-partners-with-upnorthlive-news |access-date=2024-04-05}}</ref>

=== May ===
{| class="wikitable sortable"
! Date
! Event
! Ref.

|-
! 1
| [[Comcast]] removes 18 [[Bally Sports]] regional networks from [[Xfinity]] systems throughout their overlapping footprints, in a [[carriage dispute]] between the cable provider and Bally parent [[Diamond Sports Group]], immediately affecting fans of 11 of the 12 Major League Baseball teams to which Bally Sports holds broadcast rights. The dispute injects uncertainty in Diamond's efforts to emerge from [[Chapter 11, Title 11, United States Code|Chapter 11 bankruptcy]] before the end of the year, as it had hoped to secure a deal with Comcast ahead of a June confirmation hearing with the [[United States District Court for the Southern District of Texas|U.S. Bankruptcy Court for the Southern District of Texas]]. Xfinity and Diamond Sports eventually came to an agreement on July 29, ending the dispute.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Alden Gonzalez |date=May 2, 2024 |title=Bally Sports channels pulled off lineup by Comcast |url=https://www.espn.com/mlb/story/_/id/40071329/bally-sports-channels-pulled-air-comcast |website=ESPN}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Bring Me The Sports |date=April 30, 2024 |title='Comcast expecting Bally Sports blackout |url=https://athlonsports.com/nation/bringmethesports/twins/comcast-expecting-bally-sports-blackout |website=Athlon Sports}}</ref>

! 2
| [[Allen Media Group]] announces the layoffs of up to around 300 employees across its various operating divisions (including [[The Weather Channel]], [[TheGrio]] and the company's television stations), affecting 12% of its workforce, as part of a broader effort to reduce operational costs to drive growth. Among those affected was veteran Weather Channel correspondent [[Mike Seidel]], who joined the network as an on-camera meteorologist in 1992 and transitioned exclusively to field reporting in 2012.
|<ref>{{Cite news |last=Rodney Ho |date=May 2, 2024 |title=Byron Allen's media company cuts staff at The Weather Channel, TheGrio |url=https://www.ajc.com/things-to-do/georgia-entertainment-scene/byron-allen-cuts-staff-at-the-weather-channel-thegrio/4BFNV4E2PVDY3HZ2637WIAFH5U |access-date=May 3, 2024 |work=[[Atlanta Journal-Constitution]]}}</ref>

! 5
| In a memo to [[ABC News (United States)|ABC News]] staffers, [[Kimberly Godwin|Kim Godwin]] announces she would be leaving her role as president of [[American Broadcasting Company|ABC]]'s news division, a job she had held since 2021. Debra O'Connell, the president of News Group and Networks at ABC parent [[Disney Entertainment]] (and to whom Godwin had been reporting to since February), is appointed to lead ABC News on an interim basis until a successor for Godwin is hired.

! rowspan="2"|22
| [[Mission Broadcasting]] notifies Adell Broadcasting, owner of [[WADL (TV)|WADL]]/[[Mount Clemens, Michigan]], that it would terminate its plan to purchase [[Detroit]]'s [[MyNetworkTV]] affiliate. First announced in May 2023, the deal would have seen WADL operated by longtime Mission partner [[Nexstar Media Group]] and affiliate with Nexstar-owned [[The CW]] (which the station previously carried from September to October 2023, before Adell terminated the brokered affiliation agreement in an [[local marketing agreement|LMA]] dispute with Mission/Nexstar). Though the [[Federal Communications Commission|FCC]] approved Mission's purchase on April 24, Mission withdrew the plan citing FCC-imposed restrictions that would have prevented financial backing from Nexstar from going towards Mission's purchase of WADL, limiting the amount of Nexstar-distributed programming it carried each week (likely constraining the station from carrying the full CW schedule), and prohibiting any option for Nexstar to acquire the station from Mission outright.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Jon Lafayette |date=May 22, 2024 |title=Mission Broadcasting Terminates Agreement To Buy WADL Detroit |url=https://www.nexttv.com/news/mission-broadcasting-terminates-agreement-to-buy-wadl-detroit |magazine=Broadcasting & Cable}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Keys, Matthew |date=May 22, 2024 |title=Mission Broadcasting terminates deal to acquire Detroit's WADL |url=https://thedesk.net/news/mission-terminates-deal-wadl-detroit |website=The Desk}}</ref>

| [[NewsNation]] formally adopts a 24/7 [[United States cable news|news format]] by expanding its weekend news coverage, replacing a three-hour block of ''[[Blue Bloods (TV series)|Blue Bloods]]'' reruns with an extension of the daytime news program ''NewsNation Now''. The [[Nexstar Media Group|Nexstar]]-owned cable channel that was formerly known as [[WGN America]] began weeknight news programming in September 2020 and expanded to a 24-hour all-news schedule on weekdays in April 2023, but while early-morning and prime-time news/analysis shows populated its weekend lineup, its weekend afternoon schedule had still featured entertainment programming acquired during the WGN era (notably off-network reruns like ''Blue Bloods'').
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Johnson |first=Ted |date=May 22, 2024 |title=NewsNation To Launch Weekend News Block To Complete 24/7 Programming |url=https://deadline.com/2024/05/newsnation-24-7-news-block-1235927260 |website=[[Deadline Hollywood]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Hill |first=Michael P. |date=May 22, 2024 |title=NewsNation to become 24/7 news network June 1 |url=https://www.newscaststudio.com/2024/05/22/newsnation-becomes-247 |website=Newscast Studio}}</ref>

The deal means [[NBC]]'s relationship with the French Open ended with its broadcast of the [[2024 French Open – Men's singles|men's singles]] championship on June 9; the network had broadcast the tournament each year since 1975 (save for a three-year period in the early 1980s when it aired on [[CBS]]).

! rowspan="2" |27
| The [[National Football League]] is ordered to pay more than $4.7 billion in damages after a federal jury in [[Los Angeles]] found the league had violated antitrust law in setting the price on its [[NFL Sunday Ticket]] package for games not selected for broadcast in a television market. The lawsuit covers more than 2.4 million customers who subscribed to the service from 2011 to 2022 on [[DirecTV]], which at the time had been the exclusive provider of NFL Sunday Ticket. The league indicated it would appeal the ruling if the judge does not set it aside.
|<ref>{{Cite web |date=June 27, 2024 |title=Jury rules NFL violated antitrust laws in 'Sunday Ticket' case |url=https://www.espn.com/nfl/story/_/id/40447020/jury-rules-nfl-violated-antitrust-laws-sunday-ticket-case |website=[[ESPN.com]]}}</ref>

! 24
| Eight days after its [[NBA board of governors|board of governors]] approved them, the [[National Basketball Association|NBA]] announces new 11-year deals, beginning with its 2025–2026 season, with [[ESPN]]/[[American Broadcasting Company|ABC]], [[NBCUniversal]], and [[Amazon Prime Video]]. The deals, which total $76 billion, feature a reduced NBA game package on ESPN/ABC (though ABC will keep the [[NBA Finals]]), prime time games and the [[NBA All-Star weekend|All-Star Weekend]] on NBC (a reuniting of that network with the league it [[NBA on NBC|carried]] from 1990 to 2002), streaming-exclusive games on NBC's [[Peacock (streaming service)|Peacock]] and Prime Video (with the latter carrying the in-season [[NBA Cup]]), and [[Women's National Basketball Association|WNBA]] games from 2026 thru 2036 on the same networks. The Prime Video package was matched by longtime NBA partner [[TNT Sports (United States)|TNT Sports]], who exercised an option in an existing deal that ends [[2024–25 NBA season|the 2024–2025 season]], but the league stated that TNT parent [[Warner Bros. Discovery]] did not make a "true match". WBD then filed suit against the league on July 25 claiming that they did offer a "true match". The two parties then agreed to a settlement in November, with TNT Sports retaining some international live game rights, as well as domestic non-game rights on [[NBA TV]] and the NBA's digital platforms. The 2024–25 season would still mark the end of the [[TNT (American TV network)|TNT network]] airing live games in the U.S. since 1989, and [[NBA on TNT|TNT Sports]]' overall such relationship since 1984 on [[NBA on TBS|TBS]]. In a separate sublicensing agreement, TNT's ''[[Inside the NBA]]'' studio show will move to ESPN/ABC in 2025–26.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Friend, Tom |date=July 17, 2024 |title=NBA BOG ratifies $76B media rights deal |url=https://www.sportsbusinessjournal.com/Articles/2024/07/17/nba-board-of-governors-ratifies-media-rights-deal |website=Sports Business Journal}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=2024-07-24 |title=NBA TV deal: Amazon, NBC join ESPN; TNT out |url=https://www.espn.com/nba/story/_/id/40633927/nba-rejects-warner-bros-discovery-tnt-offer-enters-partnership-amazon-prime-video |access-date=2024-09-21 |website=ESPN.com |language=en}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite news |last=Strauss |first=Ben |date=2024-07-22 |title=Warner matches Amazon in NBA media rights deal, setting up legal battle |url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/sports/2024/07/22/tnt-nba-tv-contract-amazon/ |access-date=2024-09-21 |newspaper=[[The Washington Post]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Philippou, Alexa |date=July 24, 2024 |title=WNBA secures 'monumental' media deal with Disney, Amazon, NBCU |url=https://www.espn.com/wnba/story/_/id/40634341/wnba-secures-monumental-media-deal-disney-amazon-nbcu |website=ESPN.com}}</ref><br><ref>{{cite web |last=Flint|first=Joe|title=Warner Bros. Discovery, NBA Settle Legal Battle Over TV Rights|url=https://www.wsj.com/business/media/nba-warner-bros-discovery-lawsuit-settlement-7710547b |website=The Wall Street Journal|date=November 17, 2024}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |last1=Marchand|first1=Andrew|last2=West|first2=Jenna|title='Inside the NBA' to continue on ABC and ESPN as part of TNT-NBA settlement: Sources |url=https://www.nytimes.com/athletic/5926632/2024/11/16/inside-the-nba-abc-espn-tnt-lawsuit/ |website=The Athletic |publisher=The New York Times |date=November 17, 2024|access-date=November 17, 2024}}</ref>

! 1
| [[The Walt Disney Company]] pulls its networks from [[DirecTV]] after the two sides failed to reach a distribution deal. The removal, which includes [[ABC Owned Television Stations]] and [[ESPN]], comes hours before a [[2024 USC Trojans football team|USC]]-[[2024 LSU Tigers football team|LSU]] college football game on ABC ([[KABC-TV]] in USC's home market of [[Los Angeles]] is among the stations taken off the satellite provider), and also affects ESPN's coverage of the [[2024 US Open (tennis)|US Open]] tennis tournament, as well as the ''[[Monday Night Football]]'' season premiere between the [[New York Jets]] and [[San Francisco 49ers]] (the ABC stations representing both cities, [[WABC-TV]] and [[KGO-TV]], were also impacted by the blackout). On September 7, DirecTV filed a complaint with the FCC, claiming Disney has violated the commission's good-faith mandates by predicating any licensing agreement on DirecTV's waiving any legal claims on Disney's "anti-competitive actions," including its ongoing packaging and minimum penetration demands. The blackout ended on September 14 when the sides reached "an agreement in principle". The companies are reportedly working to finalize a long-term deal.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Weprin |first=Alex |date=September 1, 2024 |title=Disney Channels, Including ABC and ESPN, Pulled From DirecTV in Major Carriage Dispute |url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/business/business-news/disney-directv-carriage-dispute-abc-espn-1235984598 |access-date=September 1, 2024 |website=[[The Hollywood Reporter]]}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=James |first=Meg |date=September 1, 2024 |title=ESPN, ABC and other Disney channels dropped from DirecTV in contract dispute |url=https://www.latimes.com/entertainment-arts/business/story/2024-09-01/directv-espn-abc-disney-blackout |access-date=September 2, 2024 |website=Los Angeles Times}}</ref><br/><ref>{{Cite web |last=Spangler, Todd |date=September 8, 2024 |title=DirecTV Files FCC Complaint Accusing Disney of Negotiating in Bad Faith as Blackout of ESPN, ABC Drags On |url=https://variety.com/2024/tv/news/directv-fcc-complaint-disney-bad-faith-negotiation-1236137207 |website=Variety}}</ref><ref>{{cite web|url=https://midmichigannow.com/news/entertainment/disney-directv-end-blackouts-working-on-new-multi-year-deal-for-carrying-channels-entertainment-sports-espn-streaming|title=Disney, DirecTV end blackouts; working on new multi-year deal for carrying channels|work=WEYI-TV|date=September 15, 2024|author=Smith, Cory}}</ref>

!30
|[[DirecTV]] announces its plans to acquire rival satellite company Dish Network from [[EchoStar]] for only $1, which would merge the two companies and would both be owned by private equity firm [[TPG Inc.]] The deal comes hours after TPG announced it would fully acquire [[AT&T]]'s 70% share in DirecTV in a $7.6 billion deal that marks AT&T's exit from the satellite TV business. The newly merged DirecTV-Dish entity would continue to support the Dish brand for the foreseeable future, meaning Dish subscribers would not be forced into using DirecTV as a result of the merger. EchoStar and DirecTV expect the deal to close in the fourth quarter of 2025, contingent on regulatory approval and bondholders writing off nearly $1.6 billion in Dish-related debt.
|<ref>{{Cite web |last=Reilly |first=Jordan Valinsky, Ramishah Maruf, Liam |date=2024-09-30 |title=DirecTV agrees to buy Dish for $1 {{!}} CNN Business |url=https://www.cnn.com/2024/09/30/media/directv-dish-network-merger/index.html |access-date=2024-09-30 |website=CNN |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=s |title=Satellite service DirecTV buys rival Dish as it fights the onslaught of streaming services |url=https://abcnews.go.com/US/wireStory/att-exits-showbiz-sells-remaining-stake-directv-private-114343562 |access-date=2024-10-01 |website=[[ABC News (United States)|ABC News]] |language=en}}</ref><br><ref>{{Cite news |date=2024-09-30 |title=AT&T sells stake in DirecTV to TPG for $7.6 bln |url=https://www.reuters.com/business/media-telecom/att-sells-70-stake-directv-tpg-76-bln-2024-09-30/ |access-date=2024-10-01 |work=[[Reuters]]}}</ref>

|[[Major League Baseball]] announces that its media division would handle production and distribution of local broadcasts for the [[Cleveland Guardians]], [[Milwaukee Brewers]], and [[Minnesota Twins]] beginning in 2025, joining the [[Arizona Diamondbacks]], [[Colorado Rockies]], and [[San Diego Padres]] as having their broadcasts produced in-house by MLB. The Guardians', Brewers', and Twins' broadcasts had been respectively carried by [[Bally Sports]]' [[Bally Sports Great Lakes|Great Lakes]], [[Bally Sports Wisconsin|Wisconsin]] and [[Bally Sports North|North]] networks, and the move comes as Bally's bankrupt parent, [[Diamond Sports Group]], announced on October 2 that 11 of the 12 MLB teams it carried in 2024 (the [[Atlanta Braves]] being the exception) would be allowed to renegotiate terms or seek new TV partners. In a separate move, MLB also announces that the [[Texas Rangers (baseball)|Texas Rangers]] would leave [[Bally Sports Southwest]], with the team reportedly seeking to create its own [[regional sports network]].

! rowspan=2|30
|[[The Recording Academy]] announces that its [[Grammy Awards]] ceremonies and related ancillary programming will move to [[Disney]]-owned [[American Broadcasting Company|ABC]], [[Disney+]], and [[Hulu]] in a 10-year deal beginning with the 2027 Grammys. The new deal means an end to the academy's broadcast relationship with [[CBS]], which has carried every Grammy ceremony since [[15th Annual Grammy Awards|1973]] and will still carry the 2025 and 2026 ceremonies.
|<ref>{{cite news |last=Porter |first=Rick |date=2024-10-30 |title=Grammys Shocker: Awards Moving to Disney From CBS |url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/tv/tv-news/grammy-awards-moving-to-disney-from-cbs-2027-1236049335/ |work=The Hollywood Reporter |access-date=2024-10-30}}</ref>

|The [[2024 Los Angeles Dodgers season|Los Angeles Dodgers]] defeat the [[2024 New York Yankees season|New York Yankees]] to win Game 5 of the [[2024 World Series|World Series]]. It is the 8th title in franchise's history and second since the shortened [[2020 Los Angeles Dodgers season|2020 season]]. The Dodgers have become the first team in Major League history to beat both [[New York City|New York]]-based teams in postseason (they previously clinched the [[2024 National League Championship Series|National League pennant]] over the [[2024 New York Mets season|New York Mets]]). As for the Yankees, they suffered a fifth inning meltdown by blowing a 5-run lead. This is the team's third World Series loss in the 21st century since [[2001 World Series|2001]] and [[2003 World Series|2003]] and first to lose at the current [[Yankee Stadium]]. [[Freddie Freeman]] was awarded the [[World Series Most Valuable Player Award|Willie Mays Most Valuable Player]] due to his grand slam walk-off during Game 1, a first in World Series history.
|<ref>{{cite web |author=Harris |first=Jack |date=October 31, 2024 |title=Dodgers beat Yankees to win another World Series, cement 'golden era' of franchise dominance |url=https://www.latimes.com/sports/dodgers/story/2024-10-30/dodgers-win-world-series-yankees-walker-buehler-freddie-freeman |work=Los Angeles Times}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Perry |first=Dayn |date=2024-10-31 |title=How Yankees blew five-run lead to lose World Series: Breaking down error-filled Game 5 that sank New York |url=https://www.cbssports.com/mlb/news/how-yankees-blew-five-run-lead-to-lose-world-series-breaking-down-error-filled-game-5-that-sank-new-york/ |access-date=2024-11-12 |website=CBS Sports |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Doolittle |first=Bradford |date=2024-10-31 |title=Freeman earns World Series MVP after historic run |url=https://www.espn.com/mlb/story/_/id/42104135/los-angeles-dodgers-freddie-freeman-named-world-series-mvp |access-date=2024-11-12 |website=ESPN.com |language=en}}</ref>
|}

!2
|Three days before the [[2024 United States presidential election|United States presidential election]], [[Kamala Harris]] appears with her impersonator [[Maya Rudolph]] in the opening sketch on ''[[Saturday Night Live]]'', a move [[Federal Communications Commission]] commissioner [[Brendan Carr (lawyer)|Brendan Carr]] claims is a violation of the [[equal-time rule]] stating that broadcasters must allow equal time to opposing political candidates. ''SNL'' creator and executive producer [[Lorne Michaels]] earlier said that neither Harris nor [[Donald Trump]] would be invited to appear during the show's [[Saturday Night Live (season 50)|landmark 50th season]]. [[NBC]] responded by filing a notice with the FCC and allowing Trump to address voters during the [[Xfinity 500]] [[NASCAR]] race and during the [[NBC Sunday Night Football|Sunday Night Football]] matchup between the [[Indianapolis Colts]] and the [[Minnesota Vikings]] the following day.
|<ref>{{cite web|url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/news/politics/elections/2024/11/03/nbc-fcc-equal-time-harris-snl/76039040007/|title=NBC files FCC 'equal time' notice after Kamala Harris appears on 'SNL'|work=USA Today|author=DeLetter, Emily|date=November 3, 2024}}</ref><ref>{{cite web|url=https://thehill.com/homenews/4968217-fcc-commissioner-claims-harris-on-snl-violates-equal-time-rule/|title=FCC commissioner claims Harris on 'SNL' violates 'equal time' rule|work=The Hill|author=Suter, Tara|date=November 3, 2024}}</ref><br><ref>{{cite web|title=NBC Gives Donald Trump Campaign Time During NASCAR Race, 'Sunday Night Football' in Response to Kamala Harris' 'SNL' Appearance|url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/tv/tv-news/nbc-donald-trump-campaign-free-ad-time-nascar-race-kamala-harris-snl-sketch-1236052709/|work=The Hollywood Reporter|date=November 3, 2024|author=Weprin, Alex}}</ref>

!14
|A federal judge approves a plan for [[Diamond Sports Group]] to re-emerge from a 20-month-long [[Chapter 11, Title 11, United States Code|Chapter 11 bankruptcy]] process, allowing the owner of the [[FanDuel Sports Network]] regional channels (which rebranded from Bally Sports in October) to continue as a viable (albeit slimmed-down) company. Coincidentally after the ruling, [[Major League Baseball]] announces that the [[Cincinnati Reds]] would become the seventh team to have their local television broadcasts produced by MLB's production arm (see also 10/8/2024 entry); Reds games had been on what was previously known as [[FanDuel Sports Network Ohio|Bally Sports Ohio]], which the Reds had a 20% interest in before selling their stake back to Diamond for $1.
|<ref>{{Cite web |url=https://frontofficesports.com/dsg-to-emerge-from-bankruptcy-new-game-plan-mlb-deals-in-place/ |title=DSG to Emerge From Bankruptcy: New Game Plan, MLB Deals in Place |last=Fisher |first=Eric |date=November 14, 2024 |website=Front Office Sports |access-date=November 15, 2024 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |url=https://www.redlegnation.com/2024/11/14/cincinnati-reds-games-broadcast-with-no-blackouts-2025/ |title=Cincinnati Reds games to be broadcast with no blackouts in 2025 |last=Gray |first=Doug |date=November 14, 2024 |website=Redleg Nation |access-date=November 17, 2024 }}</ref>

|In a unanimous decision, [[Jake Paul]] defeats [[Mike Tyson]] in [[Jake Paul vs. Mike Tyson|an 8-round bout]] at [[AT&T Stadium]] in [[Arlington, Texas]]. The fight broadcast on [[Netflix]] attracts an estimated global audience of 60 million, but also an estimated 100,000 complaints about its video quality, stream buffering, and various disconnections.
|<ref>{{cite web | url = https://www.theverge.com/2024/11/16/24298338/netflix-mike-ttyson-vs-jake-paul-fight-netflix-60-million-households-streaming-quality-buffering | title = 60 million households watched the Tyson vs. Paul fight on Netflix | first = Wes | last = Davis | date = November 16, 2024 | accessdate = November 16, 2024 | work = [[The Verge]] }}</ref>
